### P16a client write-surface guard and dead writer removal 2026-10-24

- **Scope and lineage:** branch `claude/p16a-write-guard`, based on main `f922c7349e9cc7ad699bcd878fd4ad3e9b6e923b` (#549 P15d merged). Plan `claude/project-thread-ke6672` at `3ba9611389d5f71ff5ee52be0fb954fbe2960de9` (not merged), batch P16a, Rulings R2 and R3. RED `a9a528365afb40064c3f6024c8057e8142ea8f0d`, GREEN `ac0f2f80ada0d744f314d76155cf48eefbdaf0a6`; the docs commit and the final head are in the PR body (the version authority).
- **Guard (R2):** `frontend/src/firebase/__tests__/clientWriteSurface.test.ts` reads the source text of every production module under `src/` (excluding `__tests__/`, `*.test.*`, `*.spec.*`, `src/test/` and `src/maintenance/`) and fails when `setDoc`, `updateDoc`, `deleteDoc`, `addDoc`, `writeBatch`, `runTransaction`, or a `tx|trx|transaction|batch` `.set/.update/.delete(` call appears (comments are ignored) in a file other than `firebase/cloudCommandRepository.ts` and `firebase/activeAccountLease.ts`. Four cases: the scan is not vacuous (more than 100 files, both allowed writers found, no test or maintenance file in the set); the detector recognises each write form and ignores comments and plain `Map.set`/`cache.delete`; the set of files with writes equals the allow-list; no production module imports `maintenance/` (the Node CLI writes over REST and never ships in the bundle).
- **RED (behavior):** the guard failed on exactly one file, `firebase/operatorAdmin.ts` (`setDoc`, `updateDoc`); the other three cases passed. The first draft of the guard also failed for a test-side reason (Vite glob keys are relative to the test file), fixed before the RED commit.
- **GREEN (R3):** removed `bootstrapOperator` and `setOperatorActiveStatus` from `operatorAdmin.ts` and their two unit tests, `bootstrapOperator 正確呼叫 setDoc 建立文件` and `setOperatorActiveStatus 正確更新 active 欄位`. Reason: neither had a production caller (`grep` over `frontend/src` finds only the test file), and Rules make `operators` create and update always `false`, so they could only fail against a real project; operators are created by the P15 `finalize --operators` (REST, maintainer credentials). Kept `buildOperatorDoc`, `BootstrapOperatorInput`, its two unit tests (`buildOperatorDoc 建立標準等權 operator 物件`, `buildOperatorDoc 在 uid 或 email 為空時拋錯`) and `operatorDocParity.test.ts`. This deletes dead code and the tests of that dead code; no other test was skipped, disabled or weakened. The file header now says the module writes nothing and points at the restore runbook.
- **Docs:** `grep -rn "bootstrapOperator\|setOperatorActiveStatus"` over `frontend/src`, `docs`, `scripts` and the root markdown files finds nothing except the historical P03 row of the backend plan (left unchanged as a historical record); nothing in the docs taught anyone to call them.

### P16b Rules allow/deny matrix for all 27 command kinds 2026-10-24

- **Scope and lineage:** branch `claude/p16b-rules-matrix`, based on main `f46ed1551170c4dda04a5ae41d31766f48e9feec` (#550 P16a merged). Plan `claude/project-thread-ke6672` at `3ba9611389d5f71ff5ee52be0fb954fbe2960de9` (not merged), batch P16b, Rulings R4–R6. RED `2285f8cf0e0bf7fc6ca79b2fbc477ce4b38c30ad`, GREEN `312a81f` (full SHA in the PR body); the docs commit and the final head are in the PR body (the version authority). `firestore.rules` is unchanged: this PR is test and CI only.
- **Harness (R4):** `helpers/batchCapture.ts` wraps `runTransaction` of the real `firebase/firestore` module (`vi.mock` with `importOriginal`). While armed it runs the production writer's own transaction with a recording proxy, keeps the list of `set` / `update` it wanted to commit, and aborts instead of committing; `captureBatch` fails unless exactly one transaction ran. `replayBatch` sends that same list through a raw `writeBatch` from any identity. So each step is: capture the batch the production writer (`createCloudCommandRepository`, `createCloudOrderRepository`, `createCloudDayCloseRepository`, `createCloudReportActionRepository`, `createCloudBackupCommandRepository`) would send, send it under ten kinds of wrong, then send it unchanged (positive control: must be accepted, the head must advance by exactly one and the command document must exist), then send it again (M10).
- **Mutation classes (R5), all ten run for all 27 kinds, no cell is "not applicable":** M1 unauthenticated. M2 `operators` document missing; email not verified; sign-in provider not `google.com`. M3 operator `active: false`. M4 lease held by another approved operator; lease released (`holderUid: null`). M5 lease epoch moved on; lease not renewed for 10 minutes. M6 `actor.uid` forged; `actor.email` forged. M7 one projection missing, once per non-command write (head included). M8 projections without the command; the command without any projection. M9 one extra update with the same `lastCommandId` to every existing document the command does not target (another student, a vendor, the gap day, today). M10 the accepted batch replayed. M2–M5 change the Emulator state with the Admin context, not the batch, so the batch stays byte-identical and the identity or lease is the only difference (the Rules stay the only judge; the state is restored before the next attempt). Before the positive control the spec also asserts that no command document exists and that `system/dataHead` equals its value before the step.
- **Run counts (Emulator, real `firestore.rules`, `demo-easyorder`):** 27 kinds, 549 batches sent, 522 refused (M1 27, M2 81, M3 27, M4 54, M5 54, M6 54, M7 74, M8 54, M9 70, M10 27) and 27 accepted (positive controls). Every refusal was a permission denial (`assertFails`); nothing was accepted that should not have been. Scenario order (the `step` of each kind in `rulesMatrix.ts`): CreateStudent, ChangeStudentCode, EditStudent, SetStudentStatus, SaveVendor, RemoveVendor, EnsureDay (a back-filled day), BackfillOpeningCash, StartBackfill, CloseDay, RecordPostCloseEntry, SetDayMenu, SetOpeningCash, CorrectOpening, CreateOrder, CorrectTransaction, CancelKeepDeposit, CancelRefund, CreatePayment, Undo, CreateCashMovement, CreateRefund, VoidTransaction, ReopenDay, RecordExport, RecordPrint, RecordBackup. Preparation writes that are not the kind under test (a second day, a second student, a second order, closing a day) go in through the same production writers and commit for real, outside the capture.
- **Collection matrix:** one probe per `match` block of `firestore.rules` (15, checked against the file by `rulesMatrixCoverage.test.ts`). Under five identities (unauthenticated, not on the allow-list, inactive operator, approved but not the holder, holder): `get` of one existing document and a `limit(5)` list are allowed only for the readers the Rules intend (holder only, any approved operator for `system/accessLease` and `accessEvents`, the owner for `operators`, nobody for `daily_settlements` and the catch-all) and every list under `operators`, `system` is refused; the holder is also refused after 10 minutes without renewal. The holder's bare writes without a command are refused and leave the document unchanged: update, delete, and a create that re-sends the exact content of a document that was taken away and is restored afterwards (the lease renews with a bare update on purpose, so for it only create and delete are tested). Totals: 85 reads, 75 lists, 44 bare writes.
- **The matrix has teeth (manual, not in CI):** `EASYORDER_TEST_RULES_PATH` points the spec at a different Rules file. At that point the spec had 44 tests. With `lease.epoch == epoch` removed from `commandHolder` 27 failed (`CreateStudent M5 (lease epoch has moved on) was not refused`, one step per kind) and 17 passed; with `operator.active == true` removed from `approvedOperator` 30 failed (M3 in every kind step, plus collection tests) and 14 passed; with `commands` update/delete opened to the holder and `vendors` readable by any approved operator, the two collection tests of those matches and the final completeness check failed and 41 passed. The copies of the Rules were temporary files and are not in the repo.
- **Largest legal text payloads (positive):** every free-text field was written at its schema limit (names, aliases and phone 100 characters, 10 aliases, notes and reasons 500 characters, in 3-byte characters) through the production writers for CreateStudent, EditStudent, ChangeStudentCode, SetStudentStatus, CorrectOpening, SaveVendor, RemoveVendor, SetDayMenu, SetOpeningCash, CreateOrder, CorrectTransaction, CancelKeepDeposit, CancelRefund, CreatePayment, Undo, CreateCashMovement, CreateRefund, VoidTransaction, CloseDay and ReopenDay; the Rules accepted all of them. The largest command document is 20,289 bytes (JSON of the stored document, CorrectTransaction), far below 64 KiB; EnsureDay, RecordExport, RecordPrint and RecordBackup carry no free text. Not run at maximum length: StartBackfill, BackfillOpeningCash and RecordPostCloseEntry (their 500-character reason needs a fresh back-filled day; they ran with normal text in the matrix). Their reason limit is the same 500 as in the covered kinds, but that they pass at the limit is an inference, not a measurement. The ordering student has two aliases, not ten: see the finding below. `EASYORDER_P16_MATRIX_REPORT=<file>` writes the per-kind batch paths, refusal counts and command sizes as JSON.
- **Finding, not fixed here (R1: no Rules change in P16b): a student with three or more aliases cannot be charged.** Real Rules, Emulator: with aliases `['a','b','c']` (and any length of text), `CreateOrder` and `CreatePayment` are refused by the Rules (`Unable to evaluate the expression as the maximum of 1000 expressions to evaluate has been reached`) and the writer reports `INVALID_INPUT 目前 Rules 不允許這筆命令`; with one or two aliases the same calls succeed. With 10 aliases `CorrectTransaction`, `CancelKeepDeposit`, `CancelRefund`, `VoidTransaction`, `CorrectOpening` and `ChangeStudentCode` are refused too, while `EditStudent`, `SetStudentStatus` and `CreateRefund` still pass (and so do three aliases for all of those). It depends on the number of aliases only, not on the length of names, aliases or notes. The UI (`CloudStudentsPage.tsx`) and the schema allow ten aliases, so this can happen in production. Likely cause: `validAliases` (`firestore.rules` lines 72–84, about five expressions per alias) is evaluated for the student before and after every student update. Coordinator Ruling: a P16x batch before P16c makes the alias validation apply only when the aliases change, without loosening create/edit and without lowering the cap of ten. Fixed by P16x (the next section).
- **Local verification (Node 22.22):** `npm run test:cloud-rules-matrix` (Emulator, real Rules) 45/45 passed, 0 skipped; `npx vitest run src/firebase/__tests__/rulesMatrixCoverage.test.ts` 5/5; full `npx vitest run --maxWorkers=1` 196 files passed / 18 skipped, 2,281 passed / 875 skipped / 0 failed (2,276 before: +5 coverage tests; the 45 Emulator tests are skipped without an Emulator, as for the other Emulator specs); `npm run test:cloud-backup` 24/24; `npm run lint` clean; `npm run build` passes; `tsc -b` 209 diagnostics, the same as main, none in the new or changed files. Local Emulator run time about 60 s.
- **Not in this PR:** Rules changes, list limits (P16c), the alias fix (P16x), metering, the capacity model and the P16 gate (P16d–e); no deploy, no non-`demo-` project, no production data. Rules unchanged, so no expression-budget measurement (`p05-rules-budget.md` untouched).

### P16x alias validation only when the aliases change 2026-10-24

- **Scope and lineage:** branch `claude/p16x-alias-budget`, based on main `a22c85d84af603bc3ccde06f37707b93b28f3644` (#551 P16b merged). Fixes the finding of the P16b section above, by coordinator Ruling (before P16c; the alias cap of ten is a product decision and stays). RED `c43c021` (full SHAs in the PR body), RED continued `6fdf06d`; the GREEN commit, the docs commit and the final head are in the PR body (the version authority). The only Rules change is in `firestore.rules`: `validStudentCore`, `validStudent`, `validStudentUpdate` (details and probe numbers in [p05-rules-budget.md](p05-rules-budget.md#p16x--alias-validation-only-when-the-aliases-change)).
- **RED (behavior):** `cloudRulesMatrix.spec.ts` now creates `alpha` with ten aliases of 100 characters (the Rules' worst case) so that every student-touching matrix step runs on it, and has self-contained cases for two short aliases (passes on main), three short aliases (`['a','b','c']`) and ten 100-character aliases with every kind of change (CreateOrder, CreatePayment, CorrectTransaction, CancelKeepDeposit, CancelRefund, Undo, CreateRefund, VoidTransaction). On the Rules of main 10 of 47 tests failed: the matrix steps CreateOrder, CreatePayment, CorrectTransaction, Undo, CancelKeepDeposit, CancelRefund, the longest-text case, the three-alias case, the ten-alias case and the completeness check; the two-alias case passed.
- **GREEN:** all 49 tests of the spec pass on the changed Rules (47 + the two-alias baseline case + the alias-validity case), including all 27 kinds × M1–M10 with the ten-alias student, the 522 refusals and the 27 positive controls. Every other Emulator spec of CI passes on the changed Rules as well (counts below).
- **Nothing is loosened:** the new case `aliases that are not valid are still refused whenever the aliases are written or changed` replays captured CreateStudent, EditStudent and CreateOrder batches with eleven aliases, a 101-character alias, an empty alias, a non-text alias and an empty tenth alias (CreateOrder also with a different valid list), with the command receipt changed to agree with the student so that only the alias check can refuse it; all refused. Removing the check from `validStudentUpdate` or from `validStudent` (temporary Rules copies) makes it fail (`EditStudent with eleven aliases`, `CreateStudent with eleven aliases`).
- **Coordinator Nit (matrix must not accept an expression-budget refusal as a valid denial):** measured, not applied literally. Every one of the 522 refusals of the matrix on the changed Rules, and all refusals on the Rules of main, carries the text `maximum of 1000 expressions` in the Emulator's error, because the message lists every rule evaluated for every document of the batch and the sibling rules of a refused batch run out of budget as a matter of course (the first attempt to reject that text failed 36 of 45 tests on the unchanged matrix). So the text cannot tell a check from the budget. What does: the positive control, the same batch on the same data, is accepted (the budget is not what refuses it), and now the ten-alias student makes that true for the worst case. `assertDenied` replaces `assertFails` in every refusal of the matrix: it requires a permission denial and counts the refusals that mention the budget (`refusalsMentioningBudget` in the optional JSON report). If the coordinator wants a stricter rule, it needs a way to read the failing rule, not the message; none exists in the client error.
- **Probe:** `studentUpdate` anchor `+4 / +5` (main, two aliases) → `+52 / +53` (branch, two, three or ten aliases); `cmd` `+13 / +14` both sides; table in [p05-rules-budget.md](p05-rules-budget.md#p16x--alias-validation-only-when-the-aliases-change). The probe tool got the `studentUpdate` anchor (and its README line).
- **Local verification (Node 22.22):** Emulator, changed Rules: `cloudRulesMatrix.spec.ts` 49/49 (`npm run test:cloud-rules-matrix`); every other CI Emulator spec in one run: cloudBackup 24, cloudRestore 42, firestoreRules 10, cloudRulesProof 36, cloudCommands + failure + journal 227, cloudCommands + students 200, dayVendors 10, orders 15, cash 42, refund 43, mutations 47, opening 42, dayClose 205, reports 34, reportActions 27, activeAccountLease 36, all passed, none skipped. Full `npx vitest run --maxWorkers=1` 196 files passed / 18 skipped, 2,281 passed / 879 skipped / 0 failed (the 49 Emulator tests skip without an Emulator, as for the other Emulator specs); `npm run lint` clean; `npm run build` passes; `tsc -b` 209 diagnostics, the same as main, none in the changed spec. Rules SHA-256 `488fca520bbea11ef129c4a371da831a342c2bdd27c8b1afba4ca655eaed3f6a`.

### P16d read/write meter, per-operation cost table and capacity model 2026-10-24

- **Scope and lineage:** branch `claude/p16d-capacity-meter`, based on main `a99176874ad0a6bc1c5f671036c2a56d48fc56d2` (#553 P16c merged), plan `claude/project-thread-ke6672` (`3ba9611389d5f71ff5ee52be0fb954fbe2960de9`, not merged) section P16d, R8 and R14. Tests, helpers, pure functions, a checked-in table and a CI step only: no Rules, no production code, no deploy. RED `100d921` (model test, no model), `0b4fb1b` (spec against an empty table); GREEN `1a64a1d`, `0acf6f3`, `ad484af`. Rework after review r1 (the load fits mixed independently growing collections): RED `e430e76` (model with several dimensions), `41560e5` (load fit tests), GREEN `928d940` and the commits after it; the docs commit and the final head are in the PR body, the version authority.
- **Workload assumption (CheerC, 2026-10-24):** the real traffic is under 100 people a day and hard to push past 200, so the design ceiling of the daily workload for P16d/P16e is 200 business commands a day (100 typical) instead of the 500 of the earlier validation envelope (spec §10 was changed to say so). The model and the table do not depend on it: the workload table that P16e passes in carries it.
- **What is new:** `frontend/src/firebase/__tests__/helpers/firestoreMeter.ts` (meter, wraps `firebase/firestore` and `firebase/firestore/lite` inside `vi.mock`, fake-SDK unit test `firestoreMeter.test.ts`); `helpers/kindScenario.ts` (the 27 command steps of the P16b matrix, now shared: the matrix spec runs them from there, 49/49 unchanged); `cloudCapacity.spec.ts` (Emulator, `EASYORDER_REQUIRE_CLOUD_CAPACITY`, also `EASYORDER_P16_WRITE_COSTS=<file>` to regenerate the table and `EASYORDER_P16_REPORT=<file>` for the table plus the query shapes and, in `<file>.states.json`, every measured state); `frontend/src/maintenance/capacityModel.ts` (pure functions, not in the app bundle) and `loadFit.ts` (the fit of a load), both unit-tested with hand-computed fixtures; `docs/ops/p16-capacity-costs.json` (the table, R14); CI step and `npm run test:cloud-capacity`.
- **Metering rules (what the meter counts, R8):** a document read (`getDoc*`) is 1 even when the document does not exist; a query is `max(1, documents returned)`; a request that the Rules refuse (the call rejects) is 1 read; every `tx.get` of every attempt of a transaction is 1; only the writes of the last, committed attempt of a transaction count (a refused one counts its reads and no write); a document listener is 1 read when it attaches (also when the first snapshot is flagged `fromCache`: the server sends the document anyway) and 1 per later server snapshot whose data changed. Only calls on the app's Firestore instance are counted, so the reads that a step closure does through `withSecurityRulesDisabled` (setup and checks) are not. The compat instance of rules-unit-testing wraps the modular one (`_delegate`); the meter compares the two. The query shape (collection, where fields and operators, orderBy, limit, cursor) is recorded for P16e's index coverage. The lite module is wrapped the same way and has its own unit test.
- **Rules read authorization (not metered by the SDK):** counted per read request from the Rules, with no cache (conservative): `operators/*` 0, `system/accessLease` 1, `accessEvents` 1, every other read 2 (the operator and the lease through `currentHolder`). `readAuthorizationDeduped` is the same number: the two documents of a request are distinct, so nothing is shared inside one request, and the cross-request sharing that Firestore may apply is not assumed. (The pricing page could not be read in this environment past the rules billing text; the dedupe is therefore an estimate and the gate uses the conservative figure.) The commit request is not measured by the SDK either: its Rules access calls are the per-kind figures of `p05-rules-budget.md` (see `rules.<Kind>.source` in the table; `StartBackfill`, `RecordBackup` and kinds with no per-kind figure get the platform cap of 20, which is an upper bound), and the distinct documents of the commit are `written documents + 2` (operator, lease).
- **Per-operation table (`docs/ops/p16-capacity-costs.json`, deterministic: two regenerations gave byte-identical files):**
  - **27 command kinds**, one committed command each, through the same steps as the matrix: client reads 14–26 (`CloseDay` 26; `CreateOrder` 17, `EnsureDay` 15, `RecordBackup` 14), read authorization 16–39 (`CreateOrder` 22), writes 2–5, deletes 0–1 (`ChangeStudentCode` deletes the old code). The reads are explained row by row in `detail.reads`: `CreateOrder` = 3 × `system/dataHead` + 3 × `system/accessLease` + 3 × `operators/*` (plain reads outside the transaction, the repository's own checks; their call sites were not traced here) + 7 `tx.get` (command, head, operator, lease, day, student, transaction) + 1 command read after the commit; writes = student, day, transaction, head, command.
  - **Lease and listeners:** `acquire`/`release`/`takeover` 2 reads + 2 writes (lease update + access event) with Rules commit 6 calls / 3 distinct documents; `renew` 2 reads + 1 write, Rules commit 1; `status` 1 read; `listenerSnapshot` 1 read per lease change; the operator document listener 1 read at attach, 0 authorization (`operators/*`).
  - **14 loads that grow with history, each priced per unit of the dimensions it grows with** (`reads` / `readAuthorization` = `fixed + Σ perUnit[dimension] × units`, in the table; `detail` has the per-document and per-page parts and every raw measurement). The dimensions are what the fixture grows: `student`, `vendor`, `dayTransaction` (an order of today by one student: one transaction and its command), `studentTransaction` (the orders of one student on other days, in a back-filled day that stays open: the student's lifetime history, a separate collection from today's transactions), `dayCommand` (a command dated today that is no order, e.g. a print record), `closedDay` (a back-filled closed day), `openDay` (an unclosed past day), `settlementVersion`, `accessEvent`. Reads per unit: `students` 1/student; `orders` 1/student (the roster) + 1/transaction + 1/unclosed day; `dayVendors` 1 each per vendor, closed day and unclosed day; `summary` 10/closed day + 1/settlement version; `report` 1/student + 4.08/transaction; `accountHistory` 1/student + 2.04 per transaction of today (the range reads every student's transactions of the range) + 2.04 per transaction in the student's whole history; `auditDay` 2.04 per transaction and per other command of the day; `auditStudent` 2.04 per transaction in the student's whole history (it has no date bound, so it grows with the history, not with the day); `auditRange` 2.04 per transaction and per other command of the day, and 1.02 per student and per vendor (the commands that created them carry no business date, and the range audit reads the undated commands too); `accessEvents` 1.02/event; `dayCloseOverview` 1/unclosed day; `dayCloseState` 1/settlement version; `ledgerScan` 1.005 per closed day and per unclosed day; `backupExport` 2/command (1 query read + 1 document read each). Authorization per unit is in the table (for example 19/closed day for `summary`, 0.2/transaction for `report`). The roster of students (`orders`, `report`, `accountHistory`) is read in pages of 200 that the page-size seam does not reach, so the fit adds its pages by hand: 2 authorization calls per page (as measured in `students`) as one more page in the fixed part and 2/200 per student, plus one empty probe page read at an exact multiple of 200 (500 students are 3 pages).
- **How a load is fitted (so that every cost is attributed to the collection that causes it):** the fixture grows one dimension at a time (nine phases, each three steps of six units, all other dimensions constant) and every load is measured on the base fixture and after every step, at two page sizes (6 and 12) where the repository has a page-size seam. The slope of a dimension is read from the states after its own steps. A cost has a part per document (`b`) and a part per page (`c`: the head and lease fence reads and the Rules authorization of each page request); the slope at page size P is `b + c / P`, which tells the two apart, and the production slope is `b + c / production page size` (read from the repositories' constants by the spec). The intercept is the largest over every measured state, so the line is never below a measurement, and one extra page of every paged dimension is added to it. A load declares its dimensions and the fit rejects a load that also changes with one it does not declare (more than the run noise of 2 reads / 4 authorization calls: a scan that follows a settlement command also reads that settlement), and a series that grows faster than a line (below its line by more than the noise, 2 %, or a page step). Loads without a page-size seam (`students`, `dayCloseOverview`, `dayCloseState`) are priced as measured with one page of query authorization added. `backupExport` grows with the number of commands, which every phase grows, so its one series is every state. Because of this the model needs the units of each dimension per load (`WorkloadLine.units` is a record), and P16e's workload table has to give them.
- **Findings for P16e (nothing changed here):** a committed `CreateOrder` costs 17 client reads + 22 read-authorization reads + 19 Rules calls on the commit (7 distinct documents) = 46–58 read-equivalents, so 100 orders a day are about 4,600–5,800 reads and the ceiling of 200 about 9,200–11,600, before any page load; the 25,000 gate is decided by P16e with the workload table and the storage projection (R11). The client's repeated fences (`dataHead`, `accessLease`, `operators/*` three times each per command) are 9 of the 17 client reads. If the gate fails, P16f is CheerC's decision and no check or security validation is removed to pass it.
- **Known limits of the table:** `summary` is measured with one settlement version per closed day plus the version series of the newest closed day; `auditRange` counts the undated commands of students and vendors created in the fixture, other undated commands are priced like them; the roster pages are priced from the `students` load (2 authorization calls per page of 200).
- **Not measured separately (P16e):** the daily workload table with the 200 ceiling and the R11 gate, the two-year storage projection with real document samples, index coverage from the recorded shapes, a second tab's boot and heartbeat (it is `lease.status`/`renew` plus the loads above), and the CSV/print full reads (they are `report`, `accountHistory`, `auditRange` above). `capacityModel.ts` already has `dailyUsage`, `worstCaseWorkload`, `projectStorage` and `readAuthorizationCalls`; it is not wired to a workload yet.
- **Not in this PR:** P16e, any Rules change, any deploy, any non-`demo-` project, any production data. The independent Spark empty-project restore drill remains CheerC's local job (UNVERIFIED).
- **docs-check:** `docs-check: arch=N adr=N area=Y` — this checkpoint, spec §10 (workload ceiling), `PROJECT-STATUS.md`, `DEVELOPMENT-HANDOFF.md`.
